# Live defect register — pre-sweep, 2026-09-06

The user sweeps the whole app personally before release. **This file exists so they find as
little as possible.** Every defect an agent sees goes here, immediately, whether or not it is in
that agent's slice and whether or not it gets fixed.

## The bar for an entry

If a user could see it, it goes in. Cosmetic counts. "Probably fine" counts. A thing you noticed
and moved past counts. **Do not filter by severity, ownership, or scope** — filtering is how the
three subtitle defects the user found by watching a video for ten seconds survived every tracker.

## Format — one row, four facts

| # | surface | what the USER sees | repro (exact) | sev | status |
|---|---------|--------------------|---------------|-----|--------|

- **what the user sees** — plain language, no internals. "The list is empty after refresh", not
  "resolveCaptures returns []".
- **repro** — the exact clicks/route from app start. If you cannot repro it, still file it and say
  "seen once, no repro".
- **sev** — P0 data loss / crash · P1 feature unusable · P2 wrong output · P3 cosmetic.
- **status** — `open`, `fixed <sha>`, or `not-reproducible`.

## Rules

1. **Filing is never out of scope and never needs permission.** One line costs nothing.
2. **File it, then FIX it.** The row is written the moment you see the defect - that is
   insurance against a turn ending early. Then fix it, in order: P0, P1, P2, P3. A P3 you can
   fix in two minutes gets fixed. Leave one unfixed only if the fix is genuinely multi-turn,
   and say so in the row with what you measured.
3. **Never close a row you did not verify against the running app.**
4. **Do not delete rows.** Mark them; the register is append-only.
5. **Mint your id from YOUR OWN RANGE.** Four times on the night of 2026-09-07 two workers
   minted the same number for different defects, and twice they fixed the same defect
   independently — because both branches were taking the next lead from one shared counter and
   merging only every fifteen minutes. **Convergence is the default under that arrangement,
   not bad luck.** The ranges are disjoint, so a merge never has to renumber and a commit
   message never names an id that has since moved:

   | worker | range |
   |--------|-------|
   | `primary` | D149–D169 |
   | `primary2` | D170–D189 |
   | `backup` | D190–D209 |
   | codex | D210–D229 |

   D1–D148 are the pre-range ids: grandfathered, still unique, never reused.
   `src/shared/__tests__/presweepRegisterGate.test.ts` enforces both properties in the suite —
   the `uniq -d` check that caught all four collisions is hand-run, so it catches without
   preventing. Add a worker to `MINTING_RANGES` before it files its first row.
6. **Claim the lead, not just the surface.** The same arrangement makes two workers pick the
   same *next lead* out of one scanner's output. Before you start on one, name it in your
   class's row in a committed edit — a surface claim is not fine-grained enough to stop this.

### 2026-09-06 23:30 - A GREEN COMMIT CAN RUN RED IN `jp-wt-filesapp`. It is CRLF and the tests are not.

Not a product defect and **not something to "fix" in a test.** The full suite went
**1 failed / 14,742 passed, `VITEST_EXIT=1`** on `liquidWindowIdentity.test.ts`:
`expected '\n' to contain 'data-section={win.section}'`. That `'\n'` is the tell — the
test does `src.slice(src.indexOf('<section\n      ref={winRef}'))` and `indexOf`
returned **-1**, so `slice(-1)` handed it the file's last character.

`indexOf` missed because **the working copy of `DesktopShell.tsx` was 4225 of 4225
lines CRLF**, so the anchor is `<section\r\n      ref={winRef}` and the test's `\n`
literal cannot match. The **committed blob is pure LF** (0 CRLF lines), and
`core.autocrlf = input` converts on commit but not on checkout — so git stays quiet,
`git diff` is empty, and CI on a fresh LF checkout is green. Only this worktree runs red.

**Proof it was the instrument and not the code**, because "it's just line endings" is
exactly the excuse a real failure hides behind: after rewriting the working copy to LF,
`git hash-object` on it and `git rev-parse HEAD:<path>` returned the **same blob,
`29c4eb25`** — byte-identical to the commit — and the suite went **5/5**. So the passing
run and the committed content are the same bytes; nothing was changed to make it pass.

**Every merge-touched file here is fully CRLF** (`App.tsx` 925/925, `en.ts` 12276/12276,
`ApiKeysPage.tsx` 415/415, `ReadingLensSection.tsx` 528/528...). Only `DesktopShell.tsx`
broke a test, because only it is scanned by a suite matching a multi-line `\n` literal.
**So the next source-scanning test added anywhere in this repo will fail here and pass in
the main tree.** Before believing such a failure: `grep -c $'\r$' <file>` against
`wc -l`, then compare `git hash-object <file>` with `git rev-parse HEAD:<file>`. If the
hashes match, the commit is fine and the working copy is what needs normalizing —
never the test, and never the source.

### Environment note, not a product defect - READ BEFORE THE NEXT LIVE CHECK
Installing `7z-wasm` on 2026-09-06 (commit `9611596b`) **deleted
`node_modules/.vite/deps`**, verified absent from PowerShell as well as Bash. The
running dev server keeps its already-optimized dependencies in memory, so the
desktop, Library, Translate and everything loaded at boot still work — but any
dependency that is **dynamically imported later** now 504s.

Measured: opening an EPUB shows *"Could not open this book. Failed to fetch
dynamically imported module: .../deps/epubjs.js?v=8704ba46"*, and
`curl` on that URL returns **504**, not 404. It survives a window reload and it
survives touching `package.json`, so Vite does not re-optimize on its own.

**This is the dev server, not the product** — a packaged build bundles epubjs and
never reads this cache. Do not file it as a product defect and do not "fix"
reader code because of it. It clears when the dev app is next restarted, which
this turn deliberately did not do (another session's turn dies with it). Until
then, treat any *lazily imported* dependency failing in the live app as this,
and confirm against a fresh boot before believing it.

### Two bridge traps measured 2026-09-06 by `backup` — both read as a dead surface

1. **A regex containing backslash escapes in the `/eval` body silently fails to parse.**
   `{"js":"...split(/[ \\t\\n\\r]+/)..."}` returns *"Script failed to execute … an error was
   thrown"* for **every** call, and a `try/catch` inside the script cannot catch it because it
   is a parse failure, not an exception. Nine consecutive tabs read as broken surfaces. Avoid
   regex literals in bridge scripts — `String.fromCharCode(10)` and `split`/`join` work.
2. **`python3` is NOT installed on this machine.** A heredoc mutation control reported
   `Python was not found` on stderr, applied nothing, and the test suite then printed
   **"3 passed"** — which reads exactly like a mutation that failed to fire. Use the Edit tool
   for mutations, and always `grep` the marker back before trusting the result.

A shorter re-capture clamps the bookmark to the new last page on the reader's own `idx/(len-1)` formula (`MangaReader.tsx:973`), new pages are written BEFORE stale ones are swept so the item is never page-less, and OCR boxes are dropped only when the page count moved under them. Mutation (restore the unconditional `randomUUID`) turns exactly the 5 dedupe cases red and leaves the different-url control green; 18/18. **The user's three existing rows are left alone** — merging them means choosing which position wins, and that is their data to decide. Main-process change: unit-verified, inert until a restart. |

| D49 | reading | **19 of 20 rows in Captures were titled `clipboard` or `screen`** — the source they came from, repeated down the list beside a meta column already saying it again. The one thing that told them apart, the captured text, was never shown. The list could be counted but not read. | Reading ▸ **Captures**. Measured live: `lensHistoryList()` returns **42 entries**; the tab rendered 20 rows titled `clipboard` ×4, `screen` ×15, and exactly one real page title (*熊本県 泥棒が…\| NHKやさしいことばニュース*). Every entry had real text — **69, 24, 26, 2000, 453, 272** characters in the first six. Cause: `title: entry.sourceLabel \|\| entry.text.slice(0, 40)` reads like a fallback but `sourceLabel` is never empty here — the lens stores the SOURCE KIND in it, the literal `'clipboard'`/`'screen'` — so the `\|\|` never fired. The reader HEADING had the identical defect independently (`selected.sourceLabel`). | P2 | fixed **436ded3a** — a label that merely restates its source is treated as no label, a genuine page title still wins over a preview, and the heading now takes the row's resolved title so the two agree. Note the trap this sat behind: `readingCapturesFilter.test.tsx`'s fixture passes `'Screen'`/`'Clipboard'` **capitalised**, which is not what the lens writes, so a title test built on that fixture would have been green against the defect — the new suite is deliberately lowercase and asserts the case-insensitive comparison directly. Mutation (restore the `\|\|`) turns exactly the 2 regression cases red while the real-label and empty-text controls stay green. All 8 captures suites, 48/48. |

| D50 | reading | **Switching to a tab whose chunk was still cold showed a blank panel that said nothing.** Not a slow tab — an empty one, with no spinner, no text, and an `aria-live` region with nothing in it to announce. | Reading ▸ click **Library**, read the window immediately. Measured live: **0 panel controls and no panel text at all**, then **109 controls** on a re-read ~3 s later. Reproduced a second time on **Reading lists**. Cause: `ReadingWorkspaceView.tsx:238`'s Suspense fallback was an EMPTY `<div className="reading-workspace-loading" aria-live="polite" />` over a rule that was only `min-height: 100%` — it reserved the whole panel and painted nothing. | P2 | fixed **3cc4cee1** — it renders `common.loading`, which already exists in all four catalogs, and the rule centres it. The test pins the fallback ELEMENT by suspending the real `lazy()` Library surface, since a chunk resolves instantly under vitest and the blank frame itself is not reproducible there. Mutation (empty the fallback again) turns the regression case red. |

| D51 | reading | **Reading lists' header stacked its own toolbar and ate a quarter of the panel.** Title, two selects and New list each took a row: **155px of header on a 470px panel**, at the DEFAULT window size, before a single list is on screen. | Reading ▸ **Reading lists**, at the default 820x580. Measured live: `.rlv__head` **748x155**, selects **748x39 @y188** and **748x39 @y231**. `.rlv__head` is a wrapping flex row; `.ui-select` is a form-COLUMN primitive carrying `width: 100%` (`ui.css:263`), so in that row each select demanded the whole 748px container and no two controls could share a line. | P2 | fixed **7298006e** — verified by injecting exactly the fix into the running renderer and removing it again: **head 748x155 → 748x40 → 748x155**, selects **748x39 @y188/@y231 → 124x39 and 155x39 both @y160**. Scoped to this header on purpose: `.ui-select` is shared by every form in the app and is right as it stands; it is the toolbar USE of it that needs a width. Both `rlv__head` instances (list and detail) have the same shape and are covered. The test pins the COUPLING rather than the layout — jsdom computes none — so dropping `width: 100%` from `.ui-select` later fails here instead of leaving a silent redundant override. Mutation (remove `width: auto`) turns exactly that case red; 3/3. |

### LEAD for the next workers, not a defect row - the detector that found 3 of 5 fixes on 2026-09-06

D33/D34/D37 were all the same shape: **a catalog key that exists, is translated in ja/zh/ru, and no
app code calls it, while the surface beside it renders an English literal.** Neither guard sees it -
`tools/i18n-check.cjs` only compares the catalogs to each other, and the JSX census in `i18n.test.ts`
scores *added* hardcoded text against a baseline, so a file that was ALWAYS English stays inside its
allowance forever. **Run this for the surface you claim, before you drive anything:**

```
git grep -hoE "[A-Za-z0-9_][A-Za-z0-9_.]*" -- src/ ':!src/shared/i18n/catalogs/'
```
set-differenced against the keys in `en.ts`. App-wide it reports **1,574 of 9,851 English keys
unreferenced**.

**READ THE CAVEAT BEFORE QUOTING THAT NUMBER: it is an UPPER BOUND, not a defect count.** A key
reached by a computed template is invisible to the grep. `commands.*` reads **106 of 110 unused** and
is a FALSE POSITIVE - `commandI18n.ts:7` builds `` `commands.${id}` ``. `music.repeat.off` was the
same shape. So treat a high ratio as a place to look, and confirm each prefix by hand.

Ranked by unused count, with the ones I checked marked:

| prefix | unused / total | note |
|---|---|---|
| `ankiWorkbench` | 179 / 617 (29%) | unchecked - biggest absolute lead |
| `vnPanel` | 121 / 141 (86%) | prefix appears in 4 files, so partly real |
| `commands` | 106 / 110 (96%) | **FALSE POSITIVE**, computed lookup |
| `settings` | 72 / 1200 (6%) | unchecked |
| `media` / `agent` | 69 / 68 | unchecked |
| `grammar` | 62 / 197 (31%) | grammar is marked done - so these are likely computed or genuinely dead |
| `filesApp` | 61 / 425 (14%) | unchecked |
| `trackingSources` | 33 / 33 (100%) | **prefix appears in ZERO files** - dead section |
| `vnMeta` | 33 / 33 (100%) | **prefix appears in ZERO files** - dead section |
| `vnImport` | 21 / 21 (100%) | **prefix appears in ZERO files** - dead section |

The three at 100% with zero prefix references are dead keys, not user-visible defects: nothing
renders them, so nobody sees English. They cost bundle size and mislead the next reader. Worth a
tidy-up **after** the release, not before.

Measured live: names `Highlight in yellow / orange / red / green / blue`, each correctly `aria-pressed` (yellow `true`, the rest `false`) and each correctly repainting the note — clicking blue moved the textarea background `rgb(255,243,163) → rgb(207,224,255)`. Source: `DesktopShell.tsx:311` borrows `lens.read.color.*`, which are the Reading Lens's *highlight* verbs. | P3 | open — the wording is wrong, not the behaviour, and the honest fix is five new keys (`desktop.noteColor.{yellow,orange,red,green,blue}`) in all four catalogues. So the one entry whose visible content is a close button is also the one you cannot identify, and the desk trap that a taskbar button's centre can close the window is at its worst here. | Desktop ▸ open the **Music Widget** ▸ inspect its `.os-task-win`. ''`, and the close label falls through to `{ name: label \|\| w.section }`. | P2 | fixed fae62a5a — a new `taskName` that is always a real translated name, from `settings.mini.app.musicwidget`, which is already in all four catalogues; the visible `<span>` stays gated on the empty `label`, so the icon-only entry is still icon-only. `title`, a new `aria-label` and the close affordance all read `taskName`. Verified live after HMR: `title ""→"Music Widget"`, `aria-label null→"Music Widget"`, close `"Close musicwidget"→"Close Music Widget"`, and the visible text is **still just `×`**. **Control:** the Music entry beside it is unchanged — `"Music"` / `"Close Music"` — so the fallback did not rewrite the normal path. 2 tests; the mutation (put `title={… : label}` back) turns exactly the naming case red. |

It is decoration: it paints a waveform and carries nothing the controls around it do not already say in words, so hiding it is correct and a label would be worse — it would promise something to read. Verified live: `aria-hidden` null → **"true"**. 3 tests, including one that pins the canvas count at 1 so the four hosts cannot diverge; the mutation (drop the attribute) turns exactly the hiding case red. |

| D69 | games | **In every typed Game Arena round the answer field has no name and the next question is never announced.** The box you type into is a bare `<textarea>` - no label, no placeholder, no `aria-label` - so a screen reader says "edit, blank" and nothing about what is being asked. The question itself sits in a separate `<div class="game-prompt">` that is not a live region and is not associated with the field, so when the round advances the prompt silently swaps and the player hears only the verdict for the answer they just gave. Between the two, the six typed games (Speed Type, Kana Sprint, Kanji Reading Attack, Cloze Blitz, Listening Flash, Reverse Recall) are unplayable without sight. | Games (Game Arena) > Kana Sprint > Start round. Driven live 2026-09-06 on the running app (pid 14128, window 1). Measured on the field: `aria-label` **null**, `aria-labelledby` **null**, `labels.length` **0**, `placeholder` **""**, `id` **""**. Prompt markup read verbatim: `<div class="game-prompt"><div class="game-prompt-main" lang="ja">り</div></div>` - no `aria-live`, no `role`, no `id`. Typed `ri` through the bridge's real key transport (`sent 2 / delivered 2 / inserted 2`), pressed Check, and the round advanced **1/5 → 2/5** with Combo **0 → 1** and the prompt swapping り → る with no announcement. The whole-window control census is otherwise clean - **18 controls at rest, 0 unnamed** - so this field is the one unnamed control on the surface. `RoundVerdict` already carries `role="status"`, which is why the verdict IS spoken and the prompt is not. | P1 | fixed b459ec1f - **verified LIVE** after the mergeback landed it on the branch and the dev server reloaded: the answer box now reports `aria-label` **"Your answer"**, and the live region reads **"Question 1 of 5: り"** with `aria-live="polite"`, outside the keyed round shell (`shell.contains(live)` **false**). Control that it is not a static string: answered `ri`, and it retitled itself to **"Question 2 of 5: る"**, agreeing with both the visible prompt and the HUD's `2/5`. |

Before: all 8 buttons `class=""`, `aria-pressed` **null**, and the 4 meaning buttons `disabled: true`. Clicked the first Japanese word; after: that button alone `class="active"`, still `aria-pressed` **null** and `aria-selected` **null**, and all 4 meaning buttons `disabled: false`. So the selection is real and wired - it is only the announcement that is missing. Same defect class as D61/D63. | P2 | fixed b459ec1f - **verified LIVE** with its off-state control: before the click all eight tiles read `aria-pressed="false"` (they read **null** before the fix); after clicking the first, exactly **one** reads `"true"` and it is the same tile carrying `active`, with the four meanings going `disabled: false`. |

| D71 | games | **Every finished Game Arena round is banked twice.** One session pays double XP, writes two identical lines into "Your recent rounds", and counts twice toward the streak and the badges. `finishSession` called `recordGameResult()` - which writes `jp-game-progress-v1` and broadcasts - from **inside a `setSession` updater**, and a React state updater must be pure: React is free to run one more than once for the same transition, and StrictMode (which this app mounts through `strictRoot.tsx`) does so on every development render. Both call sites, the reveal timeout and the time-expiry effect, went through it. | Games (Game Arena) > Word Match Rush > Start round, then let the clock run out. Driven live 2026-09-06. The surface began with **zero** game keys in localStorage (`Object.keys(localStorage).filter(/game|arcade|arena/i)` returned `[]`) and 0 XP / 0 streak / 0 badges on screen. **ONE** session left `jp-game-progress-v1` holding `recent` with **two** entries - same `gameId: word-match`, same `level: 1`, same `score: 0`, same `accuracy: 0`, `createdAt` **1788725447083** and **...084**, one millisecond apart - and `xp: 10` where `stats.ts:292` pays `Math.max(5, ...)` per round. The arithmetic and the array agree: two banks for one session. Reproduced in the suite under a real `<StrictMode>`; the mutation that puts `recordGameResult` back inside `finishSession` fails it with *expected [ 2 entries ] to have a length of 1*. | P1 | fixed 0260e2fe |
| D72 | city | **The garden plays music at you without ever asking.** Open City, click the mushroom - which is the surface's ONE primary action and the only route to its dossier - and a looping ambient track starts at 35% volume. Nothing on screen says it is coming, and the On/Off switch is *inside the dossier that same click opens*, so there is no way to decline before it plays. On a study app that is talking over whatever the user was already listening to, including the app's own video player. | Driven live 2026-09-06, main tree, window "Mooncap Garden". Precondition measured first: `localStorage.getItem('jp-mooncap-music-v1')` is **`null`** - the user has never touched this setting. Opened City via `os:open`, clicked `.reading-garden-mushroom-hitbox` **once**, then read the module singleton through Vite's module cache (`import('/src/renderer/components/reading-garden/mooncapMusic.ts')`, same module key the component imports, so the same instance): `paused` **false**, `currentTime` **58.42**, `volume` **0.35**, `src` ending `mooncap-endless-dream.opus`, and `jp-mooncap-music-v1` still **`null`**. Cause: `DEFAULT_SETTINGS.enabled` is `true` (`mooncapMusic.ts:9-12`) and the hitbox's `onActivate` calls `unlockFromGesture()` (`ReadingGarden.tsx:1017`), which flips `unlocked` and falls straight through `apply()` to `audio.play()`. **RE-VERIFIED LIVE after `eba14e64`**, and this is the instrument both rows were closed with: reloaded window 1 (the singleton survives HMR, so an un-reloaded renderer keeps the old module — a stale probe handle read `enabled: true` long after the fix landed), then wrapped `window.Audio` in a recorder BEFORE opening City, so the count is taken on the COMPONENT's own player rather than on a module copy my `import()` might have duplicated. Opening City: **0 `play()` calls**. Clicking the mushroom once: still **0**. Positive control, because a fix that merely never plays would be a different defect — clicking **On** in the dossier gave exactly **1** `play()`, source `mooncap-endless-dream.opus`, volume 0.35, and wrote `{"enabled":true,"volume":0.35}`. The recorder muted on play, so nothing was audible on the user's machine, and the key was removed afterwards. | P2 | fixed eba14e64 |
| D73 | city | **Worse than D72: after that first click, just OPENING City plays music - no click at all.** Closing the window stops the track, but reopening it starts it again on its own, for the rest of the session, still with nothing written to the user's settings. | Same live session, immediately after D72 and used as its control. Clicked Close on the Mooncap Garden window and confirmed teardown really happened: desk back to **12** windows and the player's `audio` field **`null`** (`dispose()` ran). Then re-dispatched `os:open` `'city'` and made **zero clicks** - `paused` **false**, `currentTime` **1.62**, `volume` 0.35, `jp-mooncap-music-v1` still **`null`**. Cause: `dispose()` (`mooncapMusic.ts:109-117`) tears down the `Audio` but never clears `this.unlocked`, and the singleton outlives the component, so the next `configure()` reaches `audio.play()` with the gesture gate already open. Re-verified live with D72's `Audio` recorder: after the fix, the close-then-reopen sequence records **0** `play()` calls. | P2 | fixed eba14e64 |

| D74 | scraper | **The Torrent Manager tells the user their working qBittorrent is "not configured".** The qBittorrent connection card greets them with a pill reading *not configured* sitting directly beside *API key stored* and their real address `http://localhost:8080` — three statements on one row, two of which contradict the first. It stays wrong until they happen to press **Test Connection**, and "not configured" is specifically the sentence that tells a user to go and set something up that is in fact already set up and working. | Open Scraper > Torrent Manager. Driven live 2026-09-06 against the user's own configuration, and the instance was first proved to be the one holding the LevelDB lock rather than a defaults-reporting sibling: `jp-scraper-settings-v1` active profile **`balanced`**, `qbittorrent.enabled` **true**, `authMode` **`apiKey`**, `apiKeyRef` **`qbittorent`** — the real, working config, not the shipped defaults. The pin records this same connection answering `{status:"connected", version:"5.2.3", latencyMs:48}`. Cause: `TorrentManagerPage.tsx:365` renders `status ? status.status : qbit.connectionStatus`, and `qbit.connectionStatus` is a **stored** field that defaults to `'not-configured'` (`scraperSourceSettings.ts:543`) and is written **only** by an explicit test (`TorrentManagerPage.tsx:207`, `ScraperSettingsDrawer.tsx:139`). Nothing derives it from whether the settings are actually filled in, so before the first manual test of a session it is a claim about configuration made without reading the configuration. The enum already carries `'unknown'` and there is an unused `lastCheckedAt` beside it. | P2 | fixed 72523a92 |
| D75 | scraper | **The connection status is the only English text on that card in Japanese, Chinese and Russian.** The pill reads `not configured` / `connected` / `unreachable` / `unauthorized` verbatim in every UI language, while everything around it — the card title, its description, the Test Connection button, the "via API key" pill — translates. | Same card, same walk. `TorrentManagerPage.tsx:365` renders the raw enum member with `.replace(/-/g, ' ')` rather than going through `sx()`. That is exactly what the scraper's own string file forbids in its header: *"THE ONE RULE: never put a literal string in JSX… If it can be read by a user, it belongs here"* (`components/scraper/strings.ts:12-13`). The infrastructure is present and in use — `src/shared/i18n/scraperUi/ja.ts` already carries **40** `torrent.*` keys — so this is a call site that skipped it, not a surface without translation. Same line as D74, so one fix closes both. **Verified at the SOURCE, not live, and the distinction is deliberate:** the five keys are present in all four `scraperUi` catalogs (grep count **5** in each), every catalog spreads its half into `catalogs/{lang}.ts` (`en.ts:744` / `ja.ts:650`), and `node tools/i18n-check.cjs` is **exit 0 on 12,585 keys, all translated**. The live language check was attempted and **VOIDED** — see the trap on the coverage row: switching the language through a dynamically imported `i18n.ts` switches a DUPLICATE module instance, not the app's, so the whole app stayed English and nothing about the catalogs could be concluded. The control that caught it was `torrent.test`, a long-migrated key present in `scraperUi/ja.ts` as 接続をテスト, which read English in exactly the same way as the new keys. | P3 | fixed 72523a92 |

| D76 | player | **Two shelves in the Media Center library say the user filtered their media away, when the truth is they have never put anything there.** Click **Favorites** (or **Study queue**) in the library rail and the grid empties to a single grey line: *"Nothing here matches the current filter."* No filter is set - the search box is empty and the type chip is back on **All**, because picking a shelf resets it. The user is told to go and clear a filter that does not exist, instead of being told what Favorites is for or how to put something in it. Worse, the message is identical to the one a real search miss produces, so the two states are indistinguishable. | Media Center (`os:open` -> `player`) -> Library -> rail **Favorites**. Measured live on the user's own 39-item library, pid 14128 window 1: `aria-current="true"` moves to Favorites, `.medialib-card` count **0**, `.medialib-empty` text **"Nothing here matches the current filter."**, search input `value` **""**, chip **All** `aria-pressed="true"`. **Control**: typing `zzqqxx` into the search on the **Home** shelf - a genuine filter miss - produces the byte-identical string, which is the point. Source: `MediaLibraryShell.tsx:397` supplies a rich `empty` node only when `emptyLibrary` (`items.length === 0`, i.e. the WHOLE library), so every other zero-result case falls through to `MediaLibraryBrowser.tsx:305`'s single `media.browser.noMatch`. `scoped` (`:127-144`) already knows the difference: for Favorites it is `items.filter(i => i.favorite === true)`, which is 0 here while `items.length` is 39. | P2 | fixed 3835c54c - `pickEmptyShelf()` reads the pre-search, pre-chip shelf count, so Favorites now reads **"Nothing is in Favorites yet / Open any title and choose Add to favorites to keep it here."** and Study queue its own equivalent. Verified live on the same window: both, with `role="status"`. **Control re-run after the fix: the Home shelf + `zzqqxx` still reads "Nothing here matches the current filter."** - the sentence was kept, not replaced. 9 cases; two mutation controls, each failing only its own rule. |
| D77 | player | **When a search empties the library grid, nothing is announced.** The 11 cards vanish and are replaced by one line of text that a screen-reader user is never told about, because the region carries no live-region role - so the only feedback that a search matched nothing is visual. | Same window. `.medialib-empty` outer HTML measured live is exactly `<div class="medialib-empty"><span>Nothing here matches the current filter.</span></div>` - no `role`, no `aria-live`. **Control**: the Readiness pane in the same shell does it correctly - `SeanimeStudyLibraryPanel.tsx:422` renders its status line with `role="status" aria-live="polite"` and comments that it is placed before the content "so the state change is actually announced". | P3 | fixed 3835c54c - `role="status"` on all three empty nodes (filter miss, empty shelf, empty library). Verified live: the attribute reads `status` on the rendered node in each of the three states. |
| D78 | player | **The Media Center's "Subtitle position" control shows a position the user never chose, and one of its three options does nothing at all.** Every profile that has not touched it is stored on **center** - that is the shipped default - and the select displays **Bottom**, so the pane reports a setting that is not in force. Pick **Middle** and the select moves, and nothing else does: the position is not saved, and coming back to the pane shows Bottom again. There was no way to select the position the app was actually using. | Media Center -> **Media Settings** -> Subtitles & learning -> **Subtitle position**. Measured live, pid 14128 window 1, on the user's own `jp-media-player-preferences-v1`: stored `subtitlePosition` **"center"** while `select.value` read **"bottom"** (a `<select>` with no matching option falls back to the first). Picked **Middle**: after 3 s the store still read **"center"** - nothing written. **CONTROL**: picked **Top** in the same way and the store became **"top"**, so the select and its handler are otherwise live and it is this one option that is inert. Cause: `MediaCenterView.tsx:1452` offered `bottom / middle / top` while `SubtitleVerticalPosition` is `'top' \| 'center' \| 'bottom'` (`shared/playerPreferences.ts:1`) with `center` the default (`:34`); `normalizePlayerPreferences` (`:76`) rejects `middle`. The user's own value was captured before the walk and restored byte-identical afterwards. | P2 | fixed d86bcad5 |
| D79 | player | **Four dropdowns on the Media Settings pane announce only their value, never what they are for.** Default playback speed, Subtitle position, Transcription language and Whisper model all read out as a bare "1x" / "Bottom" / "Japanese" / "Tiny" to a screen reader - the words beside them are decoration as far as assistive tech is concerned. | Same pane. Measured live on all four `<select>` elements: `labels` **empty**, `aria-label` **null**, `aria-labelledby` **null**. Their visible text sits in a sibling `<span><strong>...</strong></span>` inside `.mc-setting-row`, which is not a `<label>` and associates nothing. **CONTROL**: the `<input type="range">` in the very same row pattern (`MediaCenterView.tsx:1448`) does carry `aria-label`, and the Music pane's sort select is wrapped in a real `<label>` - so the idiom was known on this surface and these four missed it. | P3 | fixed d86bcad5 |
| D80 | player | **Both diagnostics buttons on the Media Settings pane did nothing at all.** "Run player diagnostics" ran, produced a full capability report, and showed the user none of it - no summary, no panel, no toast, not even a spinner. "View report" was worse: it opened nothing, ever, in any state. Two controls sat side by side on a settings pane, each promising an answer, and neither said a word. | Media Center -> **Media Settings** -> Playback -> the two buttons under the toggles. Measured live, pid 14128 window 1, by pane text length: **2610 characters before clicking "Run player diagnostics", 2610 after** (3 s later), `[role=dialog]` **0**, toasts **0**. "View report" the same. Source: `runPlayerDiagnostics` (`MediaContent.tsx:1485`) writes a real `PlayerDiagnosticReport` into `playerDiagnostics`, and `grep -rn playerDiagnostics src --include=*.tsx` finds no renderer for it; `diagnosticsOpen` (`:475`) has **no consumer anywhere in the tree** - only its own `useState` and the setter this button calls. `exportPlayerDiagnostics` (`:1535`) was equally unreachable. | P2 | fixed d0452885 - the outcome is now reported where it is produced: a `role="status"` line reading the counts, a running state on the Run button, and "View report" toggling the export into view. Verified live on this build: before, **"No diagnostics have been run yet…"** with the View button at `aria-disabled="true"`, opacity **0.5**; after one click of Run, **"8 passed - 3 warnings - 0 failed."** and the View button live at opacity **1**; toggling it open shows **Export report as JSON** and toggling it again removes it (`aria-expanded` true -> false). **The per-check list is deliberately still unrendered and that half is OPEN**: `buildPlayerDiagnosticReport` emits **21 English prose strings** (`shared/playerDiagnostics.ts`), so rendering them would ship untranslated sentences into four locales - a real slice of ~84 catalog entries plus a key-emitting refactor, not a two-minute fix. The counts are numbers and the export writes the complete report to a file, so nothing measured is withheld from the user in the meantime. |

| D89 | youtube | **Popping the YouTube window out destroys it and gives you a second copy of your whole desktop.** Click the ⧉ "Pop out into its own window" button on the YouTube window: the window disappears from the desk, and the new OS window that opens is not YouTube at all - it is the entire Study OS desktop again, wallpaper, taskbar and every other window you had open, on the same layout. Every other app pops out correctly. | Open **YouTube** -> click **⧉**. Measured live, pid 14128: window 2's desk went **1 window -> 0**, a third OS window appeared at `http://localhost:5173/?popout=youtube`, and reading it back gave `hasTaskbar: true`, `hasDesk: true`, **`fwins: 12`** with the first two being Translate and Reading Finder - not one YouTube control anywhere. Cause: main's `POPOUT_SECTIONS` (`main.ts:1428`) **does** list `youtube`, so it opens the window; the renderer's `popoutSection()` (`App.tsx:127`) validates with `raw in POPOUT_LABEL_KEYS` and `youtube` is **the one member of `POPOUT_SECTIONS` absent from that object** - so it returns `null` and the renderer falls through to the normal desktop. The comment at `App.tsx:117` records this exact failure happening once before, to `files`, when a key was removed without its successor. `note` and `visualizer` are correctly absent from BOTH (`canPopOut = !isNote && !isVisualizer`, `DesktopShell.tsx:3853`), so they render no ⧉ and are not affected. | P1 | fixed 2e1c1177 - `youtube` added to the renderer's allow-list, the one member of `POPOUT_SECTIONS` it was missing. Re-verified LIVE on the same window: clicking ⧉ now opens `?popout=youtube` as a real pop-out - `popout-root` with a `popout-bar` reading **YouTube**, `hasTaskbar: false`, `hasDesk: false`, **`fwins: 0`**, and the pane's own content (playlists, "Check again", "Add to Plan to watch"). Before the fix the same click gave `fwins: 12` and the whole desktop. Console `/logs?level=error` **0** throughout; the stray window from the pre-fix run was closed and the desk is back to 0. Gate: `popoutSectionAllowLists.test.ts` parses BOTH lists from source and asserts they are equal in both directions, plus a non-vacuity floor of 23 and a control that reconstructs the pre-fix state and gets `['youtube']` back. It matches the renderer object by SHAPE, not by name - a concurrent track is mid-rename from `POPOUT_LABELS` to `POPOUT_LABEL_KEYS`, and the gate is verified green against both shapes. It also pins `canPopOut = !isNote && !isVisualizer`, so the two sections that are correctly in neither list cannot silently gain a ⧉ that leads nowhere. |

Source: `YouTubePlaylistsView.tsx:490` renders the row as a bare `<div onClick=…>` - `onRowActivate` (open in Video if downloaded, else toggle selection) and the shift/ctrl-click multi-select at `:493` are both mouse-only. The three per-row action buttons ARE focusable, but none of them touches `selectedVideoIds`; the only other writer is Surprise me, which picks one at random. | P1 | fixed 68bd5892 - the row is now `role="row"` + `tabIndex={0}` + `aria-selected`, with Enter/Space calling `onRowActivate` and Ctrl/Shift+Enter calling `toggleSelect`, mirroring the click handler exactly; a guard on `e.target !== e.currentTarget` stops Enter on a nested action button also activating the row. **`role="button"` was rejected deliberately**: it makes a button's children presentational, which would have traded this one unreachable control for the row's THREE action buttons. `role="row"` inside a `grid` is the one shape that allows a selectable row to contain real widgets, so both `VirtualList` call sites moved from `listRole="list"` to `gridRole="grid"`, and `VirtualList` gained `ariaRowCount` so a windowed grid still declares its true size. **NOT yet re-verified live** - the fix is on `wt/files-app` and the running app serves the main tree; the scripted keyboard pass re-run on `youtube` reached a surface with no rows loaded, so it did not exercise this. The fix is small and local: `role="button"`, `tabIndex={0}`, `aria-pressed={selected}` and an `onKeyDown` mapping Enter/Space to `onRowActivate` and Ctrl/Shift+Enter to `toggleSelect`, mirroring the click handler at `:493` exactly. |

| D94 | youtube | **Two of the five sort orders persist your choice and change nothing.** Pick "Views" or "Date" in Playlist settings and the setting sticks - it is written to the playlist and survives a reload - but the list does not move, because the app's own playlist sync never fetches either field. Every row's view count already renders as an em dash, so the surface shows the missing data and still offers to sort by it. | YouTube -> Playlist -> Playlist settings -> **Sort**. Measured live, pid 14128 window 2, on `オノマトペ`: the first five row titles before the change and after it are **identical, in order**, while the stored `sortDefault` went `playlist` -> `views` (read back through `ytList()`). Cause is data, not the comparator: of the user's **19 videos, 3 carry `viewCount` and 3 carry `publishedAt`** - and all three are on the extension-capture playlist, so **0 of the 16 in the app-synced playlist have either**. `syncPlaylist` runs yt-dlp with `--flat-playlist` (`src/main/ytPlaylists.ts:234`) and maps `e.view_count` / `e.timestamp` at `:212`/`:218`, which flat mode does not return for this channel. Dropping `--flat-playlist` would make every refresh fetch each video, so the honest fix is on the UI side, not the fetch. Store restored to `sortDefault: 'playlist'` after the measurement. | P2 | fixed b74c74dc - the status bar now states the gap when the chosen sort is `views` or `date` and no video in the current list carries that field. The predicate lives in `shared/ytPlaylists.ts` next to `sortYtVideos` rather than inline in the view, so it is unit-testable on the real shape. Two edges it gets right that the comparator does not: a `viewCount` of **0** is real data, not absence (`?? 0` in the comparator makes those indistinguishable - this very bug class), and a `NaN` cannot order anything so it counts as absent. Gate: `ytSortFieldIsAbsent.test.ts`, 9 cases, two of which reproduce the defect itself - a list with no view counts keeps its order AND the predicate reports it, then the same sort on a list that HAS them reorders, proving the comparator was never at fault. Source+unit verified, not live - same reason as D91. |

| D95 | settings | **The only way to sign in to MyAnimeList does nothing.** Settings -> API keys -> Sync -> MyAnimeList says, in the app's own words, "This account connects through your browser rather than a pasted key. **Use Manage to sign in.**" Clicking Manage flickers and puts you back on the Settings **Home** page. Cause: `MalSyncPanel` is mounted only by `ScraperPage`, which is an **advanced** page (`pageMeta('scraper').advanced === true`), and `SettingsApp.tsx:541` bounces any non-guided navigation off an advanced page while advanced mode is off. `SettingsApp` already has the exemption for exactly this - `navigate(page, settingId, { guided: true })`, whose own comment at `:191` says "otherwise the guard below immediately bounces a valid deep link back Home and **the originating control is functionally dead**" - but `SettingsController['navigate']` (`types.ts:152`) declared only two parameters, so `ApiKeysPage.tsx:321` could not pass the flag and did not. **A previous fix had already corrected this row's `managedOnPage` from `study` to `scraper`;** that made the destination right and left the button just as dead, which is why the comment there reads as closed. | P1 | fixed c3e964b3 - `navigate` accepts the options object in the shared controller type, the Manage button routes `{ guided: true }`, and a new `managedSettingId: 'mal-sync'` on the credential spec makes it land on the panel instead of the top of an 8,606-character page. Re-verified LIVE on the same window, advanced mode still **off** (`adv: false` at every read): the same click now gives **8,606 characters** on breadcrumb **Media/Scraper ADVANCED**, and reading within 600 ms of the click the card is `os-set-card **is-highlight**` - so it scrolls to and highlights the MyAnimeList panel, not the page top. (Read it late and the class is gone; the highlight lasts ~2.2 s, which is what made a first read report no highlight.) Advanced mode was toggled once during the diagnosis and restored: `jp-settings-advanced-v1` back to **null** and the `settings-advanced` class removed, both re-read. Gate: 3 cases added to the existing `malSyncSettingsRouting.test.ts` - one pins `pageMeta('scraper').advanced === true` as the REASON the flag is needed (so if scraper ever stops being advanced the test says so rather than silently over-specifying), one pins both arguments, one is a non-vacuity control reconstructing the pre-fix call. Mutation control run for real: restoring `navigate(spec.managedOnPage as 'study' \| 'scraper')` turns exactly **2 of 7 red** by name. **Ruled out and recorded so nobody re-files it:** searching "myanimelist" in default mode returns only "API keys", which LOOKS like the wrong-page defect the registry comment describes - it is not. The API keys page really does render a MyAnimeList row, and `mal-sync` is `advanced: true` so it is correctly hidden from a user who cannot reach its page. The nonsense control `zzqqxx` / `qwertyzxcv` returns **0** results, so the matcher is not padding. |

Measured live, pid 14128 window 1, on the user's own profile: of **270 visible controls** on the page, **14 had no accessible name** - 13 `input[type=checkbox]` inside `label.dict-manage-toggle` and one `input[type=text]` in a `.os-viz-row`. Source: `SettingsView.tsx:336` and `:454` put the `title` on the **`<label>`**, which names neither the label nor the input - `title` on an ancestor is not an accessible name - and the label wraps only the box, so it contributes no text either. `AiAnalysisSection.tsx:290` pairs its input with a plain `<span>`, not a `<label>`, and unlike the tags row beside it has no placeholder to fall back on. | P2 | fixed 0ba435f5 - each checkbox now carries `` `${t('settings.study.dict.useTitle')}: ${title}` ``, so the 13 are told apart by the dictionary they switch, and the folder box takes an `aria-label` from the span beside it. **No new catalog keys**: composing the existing translated phrase with a proper noun reads as a name-value pair in all four locales, and the four catalogs are foreign-dirty in this tree, so avoiding them avoids a HEAD+edit stage against a concurrent worker. Re-verified LIVE on the same page after HMR: **270 controls, 0 unnamed**, and the first four names read "Use this dictionary: Kanjium Pitch Accents", "...: JMdict (Japanese-English)", "...: JMdict (Japanese-Russian)", "...: Moedict (Chinese monolingual)". Gate: `dictionaryToggleAccessibleName.test.ts`, 4 cases - it requires the aria-label to interpolate the entry title, not just to exist, because naming all 13 "Use this dictionary" is the half-fix. Mutation control run for real: removing the attribute from the SQLite-sources row alone turns 1 of 4 red. |

'Advanced pages visible' : 'Standard pages'`, `:635` the `os-set-adv-badge` text. | D99 | scraper | **Ten scraper settings controls save your choice and change nothing, and none of them says so.** Four Developer toggles (Show Raw HTML, Selector Overlay, Record Network Trace, Verbose Timings), two Validation toggles (Verify Episode Count, Reject Duplicate Files), two Sources controls (Max Fallback Depth, Skip Unhealthy Sources), and two Torrent controls (Protocols, Verify Info Hash) are rendered exactly like the controls that work: same row, same styling, no marker, and - uniquely on this surface - **no hint text at all**. The value really is persisted and survives a restart, so the settings document is not lying; the *screen* is, by presenting a control that changes no behaviour identically to one that does. The app already has the honest answer for this and uses it on 11 other fields. | Scripted class-6 pass, `src/.coordination/presweep/inert-settings-scan.cjs`, over **48 `DEFAULT_*` objects / 446 keys** in `src/` (2,750 files indexed). The rule counts referencing files EXCLUDING the settings model - the type, the `DEFAULT_*` object, the normalizer, `fields.ts`, `*Ipc.ts`, `window.d.ts` and `featureStatus.ts`, that last one being the app's own wired-ness registry which references everything and destroys the signal. Result: **404 LIVE, 6 DEAD, 36 AMBIGUOUS**. Each of these ten was then confirmed by hand with a plain tree-wide grep: **nine have literally zero references outside their own model file**, and the tenth (`skipUnhealthy`) has exactly one - a comment in `main/scraper/sources.ts:133` describing what conflating the health states *would* do to it, which is not a read. Lines: `fields.ts` 270, 272, 278, 288, 389, 392, 435, 436, 437, 438. | P2 | fixed e870b497 - each of the ten now carries `inert: true` and a hint naming what happens instead, matching the 11 fields already flagged. **The ratchet then caught five MORE in the same class:** `performance.maxParallelDownloads`, `memoryBudgetMb`, `cpuThrottlePercent`, `reuseBrowserContext` and `prefetchNextPage` were already flagged but carried **no hint**, so a user read "Not wired" with no idea what the app does with their choice instead. Fixed the same way - **15 controls repaired, 21 inert fields now, all explained.** Re-verified LIVE (pid 14128 window 1, scraper window opened for the walk and closed after, desk **12 -> 13 -> 12**): the Developer group renders **4 "Not wired" rows** while **Sample Data Mode and Unlock Script Console beside them render none** - the control that proves the marker still discriminates - and Performance **5**, Validation **2**, Sources **2**, Torrents **2**, with **0 markers lacking a "Stored for" explanation** in any of the five. Dev server confirmed serving the edit (21 `inert: true` in the served module) with **no Vite error overlay**, so this was not a stale-module reading. Console `/logs?level=error` **0** and `?level=warning` **0**. Gate: `scraperInertFieldsMarked.test.ts`, 13 cases - it pins the ten by path, requires every inert field to carry a hint over 40 characters (so "Not wired" alone cannot satisfy it), carries a non-vacuity floor of >100 fields, and asserts four neighbouring fields are NOT inert so the marker keeps meaning something. Mutation control run for real: dropping the flag from `developer.verboseTimings` alone turns **2 of 13** red by name. **Three false leads the confirmation step caught, recorded so nobody re-files them:** (a) the five Notification toggles (`onComplete`, `onError`, `onNewEpisode`, `onScheduleRun`, `onStudyReady`) scan as 0-consumer but are **LIVE** - `isNoticeEnabled` reads them as `settings[NOTICE_TOGGLE[kind]]` (`shared/scraperNotices.ts:63`), the dynamic-access case the pin warns about, which is why the scanner reports AMBIGUOUS rather than DEAD and why a 0-consumer count is a lead and not a verdict. (b) `redactCookies` / `redactCredentials` are genuinely unread but are **not a defect**: no control is rendered for them, and `logging.redaction` is a `note` field stating redaction is unconditional with no way to turn it off - which is true. (c) **The 2026-09-06 19:00 pin is wrong** where it says `requireUnmeteredNetwork` and `fetchStaff` "DO have real consumers in `main/scraper/scheduler.ts` / `catalogue.ts`, so the app's own inert flags are wrong". Both appear in those files **only inside comments explaining they are deliberately not read** (`scheduler.ts:195`, `catalogue.ts:664`); a grep that counts comment mentions produced that claim. Audited both directions: **10 of the 11 existing `inert` flags are confirmed dead and the eleventh (`images.minWidth`) reads live only through a name collision with CSS `minWidth` in `appZoom.ts` / `liquidReadingCanvas.ts` / `studyWorkspace.ts`.** The app's self-flagging was accurate; the gap was the ten unflagged ones. |

| D100 | cross-cutting | **283 user-facing strings stay English in every language, in 41 files that ARE translated - and the repo's own i18n guard reports clean on all of them.** `tools/i18n-hardcoded-check.cjs` asks whether a component *adopts* i18n at all: does it call `t()` anywhere. That is the right question for an unconverted surface and the wrong one for this defect, so a file that resolves nine labels through `t()` and renders the tenth as a literal passes it. That is drift, and it is what a user sees, because the untranslated line sits directly beside a translated one. **The worst of it is the Start menu**, the most-seen chrome in the app: on the Aero start panel `title="Search"` and `aria-label="Search"` (`DesktopShell.tsx:3177-3178`), the section labels `aria-label="Study programs"` (`:3189`) and `aria-label="Places and tools"` (`:3207`), and the whole power cluster - `aria-label="Secret OS power"` (`:3250`) plus **Sleep / Restart / Shut down Secret OS each as both `title` and `aria-label`** (`:3254-3273`). A Japanese screen-reader user hears "Shut down Secret OS" in English. | Scripted class-3 pass, `src/.coordination/presweep/partial-i18n-scan.cjs`: **430 `.tsx` scanned, 41 call `t()` and still render an untranslated user-facing string, 283 strings total.** It scores only positions a user reads - `label`/`placeholder`/`title`/`aria-label`/`alt` attributes, `label:`/`hint:`/`description:` object properties, and JSX text children - and masks comments and `t(...)`/`sx(...)` arguments so neither can score. **Validated against the two known cases before any new output was trusted**: it reproduces **D92** (7 of its strings in `YouTubePlaylistsView.tsx`, including all five field labels and both placeholders) and **D98**'s `Advanced`. Top files: `DesktopShell.tsx` **39**, `PlaylistEditor.tsx` 21, `StatisticsView.tsx` 21, `ResourcesView.tsx` 20, `MiniShell.tsx` 17, `CalendarView.tsx` 17, `NovelReader.tsx` 17, `SettingsApp.tsx` 16, `MediaStudyMode.tsx` 15, `ImmersionView.tsx` 12. `SettingsApp.tsx` shows **D98 is understated**: not 7 strings but the entire menu bar - File, View, Page, Control Center Home, Find a setting, Open Display, Open Lockscreen, Desktop layout, Keyboard shortcuts, Appearance, Reading, Transcription, Memory and storage, Special modules, and `aria-label="Settings commands"`. | P2 | open - **genuinely multi-turn, and that is why it is filed rather than half-fixed.** 283 strings across 41 files needs ~283 keys x 4 locales, and all four catalogs are foreign-dirty in this tree. **The exact next slice is the 9 distinct Start-menu strings in `DesktopShell.tsx`** (the 11 sites above; Sleep/Restart/Shut down each supply one string to two attributes): highest traffic, smallest key count, and `DesktopShell.tsx` is currently **clean** in this tree so only the catalogs need HEAD+edit staging. **Two things the next worker must NOT treat as defects without deciding first:** (a) the 26 `hint:` values at `DesktopShell.tsx:272-298` are the **Wired theme's flavour table** (`SIG-LIB` / "Signal Library" / "Local media catalog"), themed data rather than chrome - decide whether Wired's fiction is translated at all before converting them, and record the decision; (b) `Ctrl P` and similar are key names, legitimately literal. The scanner's own output says a hit is a lead. **An instrument bug found and fixed before publishing any of these numbers, worth knowing:** blanking a multi-line block comment with `' '.repeat(len)` deletes its newlines and silently shifts every reported line number below it - the first run pointed at `:2980` for a string that is on `:3177`. Newlines are preserved now and the eleven line numbers above were each read back off the file. |

| D107 | cross-cutting (desktop shell, media, wallpaper) | **Twelve confirmation and alert dialogs are English no matter what language the app is in — and four of them are destructive.** Reset desktop, Shut down Secret OS, Clear media library and Delete playlist each stop and ask before they take something away, and in Japanese, Chinese or Russian the entire question, the explanation of what will be lost and the confirm button all render in English. A user who cannot read them is choosing blind on the one screen that exists to stop them choosing wrong. The Clear media library message is the sharpest case: its three sentences are the only place the app says the original files on disk are NOT deleted. **The same defect runs through the Secret start menu around them** — Search, Study programs, All programs, Places, Tools, Control panel, Personal study desktop and the whole Sleep / Restart / Shut down power cluster, as both visible text and as the accessible names a screen reader announces. | Switch the UI language to 日本語, open the Secret start menu, then Media Center → Clear library, or Settings → Living atmosphere → Delete playlist. **Found by source scan, extending the class-3 pass rather than by a live walk, and the reason matters: neither existing guard can see this class.** `tools/i18n-hardcoded-check.cjs` asks whether a file *adopts* i18n at all, and `DesktopShell.tsx` and `PlaylistEditor.tsx` call `t()` hundreds of times; `partial-i18n-scan.cjs` (D100) scores `label:`/`hint:`/`description:` object properties and JSX text, not `title:`/`message:`/`confirmLabel:`, and cannot see a string inside a `wired ? ... : ...` ternary either. Counted repo-wide: **39 dialog call sites, 12 of them passing raw literals** across `DesktopShell.tsx` (7), `PlaylistEditor.tsx` (4) and `MediaLibraryActions.tsx` (1). `MediaLibraryActions.tsx` was fully unconverted — baselined at 10 strings, calling `t()` nowhere. | P1 | fixed 90b5f002 + 40b9e06b — **53 keys x 4 locales across the two commits**, and the scanner's own count for `DesktopShell.tsx` went **39 → 28**. Three things worth more than the count: (a) `MediaLibraryActions.tsx` is converted whole and **removed from `tools/i18n-hardcoded-baseline.json`**, so the repo ratchet tightens from 31 files / 778 strings to **30 / 768**; its hand-rolled `entry`/`entries` plural is gone, both counts now use CLDR arms with ru carrying one/few/many/other, and both `useCallback`s gained `lang` per the repo rule. (b) **The remaining 28 hits in `DesktopShell.tsx` are a recorded DECISION, not a backlog**: `WIRED_MODULES` is the Wired theme's in-fiction module table (`SIG-LIB` / `Signal Library` / `Local media catalog`) and the three wall-atmosphere spans are `aria-hidden` decoration — themed content, which the repo's own scope rule keeps out of the catalogs, exactly as deck names and glosses are. The 28th is `Ctrl P`, a key name. D100 flagged this as needing a decision before conversion; this is the decision, so nobody re-proposes it. `playlistEditor`'s `defaultValue: 'My playlist'` is left literal for the same reason and now says so in a comment. (c) **Gate `dialogTextTranslated.test.ts` pins the RULE repo-wide, not the twelve sites** — it walks every `.ts`/`.tsx` outside tests, reads each dialog call's brace-balanced argument object (so a long `message` cannot push `confirmLabel` out of a fixed line window) and rejects **template** literals as well as quoted ones, because a `message` interpolating a playlist name was one of the real defects and a single-quote scan would have passed it. 5 cases: a non-vacuity floor of 40 fields, a check that the three repaired files still raise dialogs at all so the test cannot go green by deletion, and a control in each direction. **Mutation control run for real:** restoring one raw `title` in `PlaylistEditor.tsx` turns it red and names the file and line. **Source- and gate-verified, NOT live** — the running app serves the main tree and these commits are on `wt/files-app`; the new keys do not exist in the running renderer's catalogs, so `translate()` there cannot resolve them. Re-verify after the mergeback by switching the UI language and reading the start menu. **Still open and named for the next worker:** D100's other 40 files, of which `PlaylistEditor.tsx` keeps 21 — its `CAL_CATS` label table is the module-level-array case that needs an i18n *key* per entry, not a `t()` call at declaration time. |

So any turn touching one of those files had to prove the redness was foreign before it could read its own result; this turn spent a step on exactly that. **The half that matters more is invisible:** the rule never ran, so those dependency arrays were never checked, while the comment beside each claimed a deliberate exemption from a check that does not exist — and most of them are the `lang`, never `t` case CLAUDE.md §6 calls the number-one review item for new i18n code. **Four files had already worked this out** (AnkiCardPreview, DictionaryResults, WiredLyricPreview, WiredLyricStream) and written the corrective note. | P2 | fixed `95d4803f` — the other six brought onto the existing convention, 10 directives, every explanation kept. `npx eslint` on all ten: **13 errors → 0**. (2) `dictService.japaneseCharacterReadings` drops non-kana readings from a `ja` row on the read path, which **repairs the 13,108 rows already on this machine without a re-import** and is a no-op once the import is clean; single call site (`lookupCharacter`), single caller, so no blast radius. Applying the filter to the real DB: 食 → `ショク · ジキ · く.う · く.らう · た.べる · は.む`, 犬 → `ケン · いぬ · いぬ-`, 37,459 readings kept / 49,039 dropped. 403 rows keep nothing — CJK-extension characters KANJIDIC2 carries with only Chinese readings, exactly **1** of which has any JLPT level, grade or frequency; they return empty, which is what a clean re-import stores (349 rows already do) and what the panel hides its Readings line for. **8 tests, 3 mutation controls, each caught by name and nothing else** (revert the reading filter → 2 fail; revert the meaning filter → 3 fail; disable the read-path filter → only the repair test fails). **NOT verified live: this is main-process code and `primary2` was mid-turn, so the shared app must not be restarted.** The before/after above is the same filter run over the same rows by a read-only script; the next turn that restarts the app should re-open 食 and confirm. **The meanings half is import-time only** — `chars.meanings` has no language tag to filter on, so the French/Spanish/Portuguese glosses clear on a KANJIDIC2 re-import (Settings → Models & dictionaries → Import dictionaries) and not before. |

The reachable-for-real case is a Windows clipboard lock held by another process, which is a known transient `writeText` rejection; I did not reproduce that. | P3 | **open, and deliberately not fixed this turn.** A bare `.catch(() => {})` would silence the unhandled rejection and *keep* the lie, which is the silent-catch class the sweep is looking for, not a fix for it. Do not close it by swallowing the rejection. |

**Considered on `dictionary` and REJECTED, recorded so nobody re-files it:** "Forget this explanation"
(`EntryExplain.tsx:158`) destroys a cached AI answer on one click with no confirm and no undo, and its

explanations *across every prose language, model and version*, while the button reads as singular. The
user's `explanations` table holds exactly **1** row (`ja/食べる`, `cloud:gemini-2.5-flash:default`), so the
scope mismatch is not observable on this machine, and **`Explain again` sits directly beside it**, so
recovery is one click. A modal on a regenerable cache would make the surface worse, not more honest. Not
a defect. (`Explain again` itself was deliberately NOT driven: it spends the user's cloud API credit.)

Only 2 shows are real (`c:/users/arseniy/downloads/…`, 106 s and 107 s). Eleven of the fourteen day rows are seeded too, each with `chars` an exact 11× or 12× multiple of `seconds`; the three real days are 09-05, 09-06 and 09-07, which carry fractional seconds. The two ledgers therefore disagree — days say 7,173 s watched while shows say 8,813 s, and days say 542,290 chars read while books say 448,790 — **and that disagreement is the seeding, not a product bug**, since `recordReading`/`recordWatching` both write the day and the book/show in one call with the same value. A marker scan of all 71 localStorage keys finds the contamination in exactly **three**: `jp-study-stats-v1-ja`, `jp-flashcard-deck` (4 cards from `import-zzprobe-headerless`) and `jp-deck-level-cache-v1` (the same import's row). This needs the user's word, because the fix is deleting data from their profile and there is no restore point.** The cleanup is mechanical and I have already identified every row: drop `books.bk-seed-book-1..8`, drop the four `shows` whose key starts `file:D:/`, drop the eleven day rows whose `chars` is an exact integer multiple of `seconds`, and drop the four `jp-flashcard-deck` cards plus the one `jp-deck-level-cache-v1` row whose `bookId` is `import-zzprobe-headerless`. | D250 | player / video — automatic subtitles | **A file the library read wrong when it was imported is read wrong forever, even after the parser learns to read it.** The user's own library carries `[Anime Land] JoJo no Kimyou na Bouken - Ougon no Kaze 39-END … .mp4` with **no episode number at all** and a series key of its own — `jojo no kimyou na bouken ougon no kaze 39 end` — so the final episode of Golden Wind sits outside its own series. Today's parser reads that name correctly; the library was written before it could, and nothing ever asks it again. Subtitle discovery cannot request "episode 39" for a file that does not know it is episode 39. | Live, the user's real `media.json` (39 items). Group the library by `seriesKey`: Golden Wind appears **twice**, once as `…ougon no kaze` (episode 38, kind `episode`) and once as `…ougon no kaze 39 end` (episode **undefined**, kind `unknown`). Re-parsing every stored `fileName` through the CURRENT `parseMediaFileName` and comparing only the four fields that parser owns (`seriesKey`, `season`, `episode`, `episodeKind`) changes **exactly 1 of 39 items** — that one — to `seriesKey: …ougon no kaze`, `episode: 39`, `episodeKind: episode`. `mediaFileIdentity.ts` says so in its own words: the `-END` tail was added because "without it the final episode of a season is the one file the rule cannot read", and `providerSearchTitle`'s doc names this exact file as "added before the parser could read a `-END` tail". The repair never ran, because `media.ts:1078` and `:209` both gate the backfill on `item.seriesKey === undefined` — a *wrong* key is not an absent one. | P2 | **fixed — this commit.** `RELEASE_IDENTITY_VERSION` in `mediaFileIdentity.ts`, stamped on each item, drives a one-time repass in the `media:list` backfill. **It re-derives only the four fields the parser is the sole author of** — verified by grep that no provider writes `seriesKey`, `season`, `episode` or `episodeKind` — and deliberately does **not** touch `seriesTitle`, `year`, `category`, `releaseGroup` or `resolution`, because `mediaMetadata.buildPatch` writes `seriesTitle` and `year` from AniList/Jikan and a repass would overwrite "JoJo's Bizarre Adventure: Golden Wind" with the release name. **Verified by running the SHIPPING function over a copy of the user's real `media.json`:** 39/39 stamped, fields moved on exactly **1**, a second pass touches **0** (idempotent), Golden Wind collapses from **2 series keys to 1**, and that item's `seriesTitle` and `year` come out byte-identical. 8 tests appended to the existing `mediaFileIdentity.test.ts` harness (no new probe), **3 mutation controls, all caught by name** — restore the old `seriesKey !== undefined` gate (4 fail), add `seriesTitle` to the owned list (2 fail, including the provider-safety test), drop the never-unsets guard (1 fail). **Main was NOT restarted, so the repair has not yet run in the user's live app** — it fires on the next `media:list` after a restart. Not restarted deliberately: a restart on this profile has a recorded history of destroying renderer `localStorage` (2026-09-03), and D169's contaminated-stats evidence lives there. |
## Coverage - the user starts THEIR sweep when this table is full

They are waiting on this. Claim a surface by putting your worker name in `by` BEFORE you start,
so two workers never take the same one. Fill `done` only when you have driven every control on it,
watched the console throughout, and filed every row you saw. `found` is how many rows that surface
produced - **0 is a legitimate answer and must be written, not left blank**, because a blank reads
as "not visited" and costs the user a second pass.

The merge resolved to `primary`'s shape in both cases and mine were withdrawn, so the surviving ids are **D147** (the folder half of the app drawer, which theirs did not cover) and **D148** (the Media Hub i18n, which is not a class-5 row at all). **`git rev-list HEAD..feat/nyaa-subtitles` read 0 at the top of my turn and was correct then; the branch moved while I worked. Re-check the tip before the LAST commit as well as the first.** **THREE CORRECTIONS TO THIS ROW, all measured:** (1) *"Comments and string bodies are masked so neither can supply a fake guard"* was **never true** — the scanner ran its regex over raw text. It scored a doc comment naming a call as a call site (a phantom `immersionRemoveSite` at `immersionSiteActions.ts:4`), and, far worse, a comment mentioning `confirmDialog(` inside an enclosing block would have made an unguarded call read as GUARDED — a false clean bill that nothing would have surfaced. Comments are masked as of `e513d56d`, length-preservingly so line numbers stay real; string bodies are deliberately **not**, and this row now says so. The same repair is in `destructiveGuards.test.ts`, where the bug announced itself by **throwing** instead of failing. (2) **The gate shape that closes the class, not just the defect: `SOLE_CALLERS`.** D137, D142/D143 and D145 are all one defect — a second host reaching past a guarded path — and a per-call-site ratchet cannot catch the next one, because the next one is a NEW file. ### The two scripted class passes, 2026-09-06 by `primary2` - method, numbers and limits

Both run from one parameterised harness, `src/.coordination/presweep/a11y-name-scan.cjs`,
driven through the debug bridge against pid 14128. **24 of the 25 surfaces**: the user's
12 open windows were scanned READ-ONLY in window 1 (real data, real state, nothing
disturbed), and the other 12 were opened one at a time on the empty desk in window 2 and
closed again - both desks ended at exactly the count they started at. `note` is excluded
deliberately: it needs content to be worth scanning and its close button is a delete
confirm.

**Pass 1, accessible name + state.** `0` unnamed controls across all 24 surfaces. That is
the headline: the class that produced 49 of the first 89 findings is genuinely closed by
the manual walks, not merely counted. What it DID find is the other half of the class -
**5 live sites** where a button looks chosen and announces nothing (D95 library, D96
novels x2, D97 youtube x2), which a source sweep then widened to **20 sites** in three
files. Two of those files carried no aria state attribute at all.

**Pass 2, keyboard reachability.** Across 24 surfaces and ~2,140 clickable-looking
elements, **1** finding (D98) and **0** positive tabindex.

**The instrument was wrong four times before it was right, and that is the useful part.**
The first run reported 17 findings; 16 were the harness, not the app:
  - a **disabled** control (the Media Center transport with nothing playing) is not
    mouse-only - it cannot be operated by either input. That alone was 9.
  - `<summary>` is natively focusable and was missing from the focusable set, so every
    collapsed disclosure on the rail read as a trap.
  - **roving tabindex is CORRECT.** The Reading workspace tablist
    (`ReadingWorkspaceView.tsx:215`) is textbook - `role="tab"`, `aria-selected`,
    `tabIndex` 0 on the active item and -1 on the rest, arrow keys wired - and scoring it
    would have been a false report against good code. 6 of 7 second-run findings.
  - a `<span>` inside a `<label>` is operated through the label's own control.
Read the source before scoring a keyboard finding. Every one of those four would have
been a defect row filed against working code.

**What these passes do NOT cover, stated so the count is not read as more than it is:**
only each surface's state AT REST is reached - controls behind a tab, a closed
`<details>` or a dialog were counted separately and not scanned (11-25 per surface on
the denser ones). Focus-ring visibility, Escape-closes-every-dialog and tab ORDER are not
measured by either pass and remain manual. And `youtube`'s keyboard re-run landed on a
surface with no video rows loaded, so it did **not** re-measure D91.

**When every row has `done`, say so plainly in the handoff** - "AI sweep complete, N surfaces,
M defects, K fixed" - so the user knows their pass can start.

### 2026-09-07 00:30 EDT - THE APP WAS LEFT IN RUSSIAN, AND IT IS BACK IN ENGLISH

pass". Every surface the user opens for their own sweep would have been in Russian.

for free in a genuinely switched app (the leads are in the cross-cutting row), and **D120** was
found - which is a defect that is only visible in a non-English UI.

not by writing `localStorage`: a dynamic `import()` of `i18n.ts` from the bridge mutates a
DUPLICATE module instance and flips `documentElement.lang` while the app stays where it was.

settings rail `Внешний вид → Appearance`.

surface walk in this register reads English, and a worker recorded setting it.

### 2026-09-07 00:25 EDT - THE MERGE-BACK LEFT CONFLICT MARKERS IN THE MAIN TREE AND BROKE THE APP

Not a product defect, but it cost this turn twenty minutes and it will happen again, so it is
written down here rather than in a handoff nobody re-reads.

**What happened.** `wt/files-app` was landed onto `feat/nyaa-subtitles` as a clean fast-forward
(main-tree HEAD moved to `55c74450`). But two files the incoming commits touch were **dirty in the
main tree with another track's uncommitted i18n work**, and the landing wrote `<<<<<<< ours` /
`>>>>>>> theirs` markers straight into the working copies of
`src/renderer/components/settings/pages/VerifiedSitesManager.tsx` (3 blocks) and
`src/renderer/components/MediaLibraryActions.tsx` (5 blocks).

**How it presents, and why it is easy to misdiagnose.** There is **no `MERGE_HEAD` and no `UU` in
`git status`** - both files read as an ordinary ` M`, so every "is the tree mid-merge" check says
no. The only symptom is that the shared dev app stops working: Vite's babel plugin fails on
`Unexpected token` at the `<<<<<<< ours` line, `document.querySelector('vite-error-overlay')` is
non-null, and **`os:open` silently opens nothing** - which reads exactly like a dead surface. Two
`os:open` calls were spent on "settings will not open" before the overlay was read.

**Detection, one line, cheap enough to run at the top of any turn that drives the app:**

    grep -rln "^<<<<<<< ours" src/

**Resolution.** Both sides were wanted and neither belonged to one track alone: the incoming half
added `LANG_TAGS[lang]` to a date, the dirty half was translating the same panel. The union is the
answer - keep both imports, take the translated string AND the locale argument. Resolved by hand in
the main tree working copy and deliberately **left uncommitted**, because the surrounding hunks are
the other track's to commit. `MediaLibraryActions.tsx` cleared itself between two greps ninety
seconds apart, so something automated is also repairing these; do not assume you are the only
writer, and re-grep before editing.

### 2026-09-06 20:35 EDT - COLLISION NOTE, read this before merging wt/files-app

`primary` (main tree) and `primary2` (`wt/files-app`) ran the class-5 destructive-guard pass in
the SAME HOUR without seeing each other, and **two of the three fixes are the same fix**. Nothing
is lost and neither side is wrong; the merge needs one decision per file, and this note is so
nobody has to re-derive it.

| action | main tree | wt/files-app |
|---|---|---|
| lens `Clear all` | D111(a), `a96badce` | D109, `45a1aa5a` + `46ac1ab2` |
| API keys `Remove` | D111(c), `a96badce` | D108, `f7993ef2` |
| Monitors `Reset` | D111(b), `a96badce` | **not fixed - main tree only** |
| the scanner | `src/.coordination/presweep/destructive-guard-scan.cjs` | **same path**, 187 lines, different code |

**Keep ONE guard per action.** Two `confirmDialog` calls in one handler asks twice. The key names
differ on the two sides, so after picking a side, delete the loser's keys from all four catalogs -
`i18n-check` stays green with dead keys and will not catch them.

**On the lens message, take the wt/files-app WORDING and the main-tree COUNT.** Their prose is
better ("This clears everything, not just what the current filter shows"), but their
`{ count: entries.length }` is **the filtered page, not the store** - `lensHistoryList` is called
with a limit, so a user with 200 captures and a filter showing 12 is asked to "Delete all 12 saved
captures" and loses 200. That is a count-vs-truth defect inside a class-5 fix. Either drop the
count or add a real total from main; do not ship `entries.length`.

**Root cause, and it is fixable.** The 16:20 pin says to check `rev-list --count
feat/nyaa-subtitles..wt/files-app` before claiming a surface. That was done (10 commits) and the
D-number range was read off the other branch too - but only the NUMBERS were read, not the other
branch's cross-cutting table, and class 5 is a cross-cutting pass rather than a surface. **Before
claiming a cross-cutting CLASS, read `wt/files-app`'s cross-cutting table, not just its coverage
table and its highest D number.**

   `const SHELL = 'src/renderer/components/DesktopShell.tsx'`, so 45 sites in 20 other files
   were outside its universe while it reported green.

   `onClick={() => …}` before the end of the opening tag, so **any button with a callback was
   skipped — including one in the file it *was* scanning** (`DesktopShell.tsx`'s Remove from
   desktop, which codexA fixed as D122). Switching to a TSX parse moved the count from 45 to 52.

**Method that made it one turn rather than twenty:** 32 of 45 already carried a `title` saying
the right words, so those are a scripted copy — the string was never the missing thing, only its
attribute. The remaining 13 had no words anywhere and were hand-written, which is where the 9
new keys went. Move/remove in a list now name the row they act on (`Move {name} up`) rather than
repeating "Move up" six times down a column.

## 2026-09-07 01:05 EDT — backup: recovering codexB's interrupted slice, and a third id collision

**codexB ended mid-turn at 00:58:16** (usage limit), between `04188419` and a commit of its own.
It left a coherent, finished-looking slice uncommitted: `stats.ts`, `StatsContent.tsx`,
`widgets/study.tsx` and an untracked `statsLiveUpdates.test.tsx`. Recovered and landed whole as
**`1ba582b7`** — nothing was rewritten, only verified and committed.

**D126 fixed.** `useStats`' summary memo depended solely on a manual refresh nonce, so an open
Statistics window subscribed to nothing at all. `onStatsChanged()` is now the one subscription
both hosts share: the reading and watch events, a new `STATS_RESET_EVENT`, the study-language
change, a cross-window `storage` write to the **active** stats key, window focus, and a 60 s clock
that refreshes only when the day key actually rolls over. The study widget's hand-rolled listener
pair plus its unconditional 30 s poll became the same one call, so it no longer repaints on a
timer. Read and watch stay independent channels; no number is computed differently.

**Verified, not assumed.** `statsLiveUpdates` + `statisticsModeParity`: **11 passed, 0 failed**.
**Mutation control run for real** — deleting the `useStats` subscription line fails **4 of the 5**
new cases, naming the midnight-rollover assertion first; restoring it returns 5/5. The disposal
case is the one that would have caught a leak: after `dispose()` it drives a real `recordReading`,
a `focus` and 24 h of fake time and asserts `refresh` was never called and `getTimerCount()` is 0.

**Three defect ids collided, not two.** codexB had already renumbered its own two rows in the
working tree (`D123`→`D127` Aero watch history, `D124`→`D126` this row) because `primary` had
committed D123/D124/D125 for the mojibake and glyph-button classes in the same minutes. It did not
reach the third: **`D107` is used twice** — line 279 is primary2's twelve raw-literal dialog call
sites (fixed `90b5f002`+`40b9e06b`), line 386 is codexB's shared `dialogService` **defaults**
(open). Different defects, same id. The later row is renumbered **`D128`**; the earlier keeps
`D107`, since three other passages already cite it by that number. Both are real and neither is
merged into the other. The register now has **zero duplicate ids** (`grep -o '^| D[0-9]* |' | uniq -d` is empty).

**Why this class of collision keeps happening, for whoever files next:** three workers append to
one register concurrently and each picks "the next free number" from a copy that is already stale
by the time it writes. The cheap defence is to run that `uniq -d` after appending a row, which is
how this one was found.

**Gate footnote for D123/D124, 2026-09-07 01:00.** The first full run at `e1985518` came back
**1 failed / 14,751 passed** and the failure was **my own new gate**, not the product: reading
3,500 files timed out at vitest's 20s default under parallel load while passing in ~1s alone.
